Smask a run's secrets in a method's logger and console output on stdout in log mode and under --log (swamp-club#4412) (#3625)

Security-related change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-05T02:39:29Zsecretsfix

What's Changed

  • fix(secrets): mask a run's secrets in a method's logger and console output on stdout in log mode and under --log (swamp-club#4412) (#3625)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#4412. In swamp behavior run and swamp workflow run in log mode, a secret a method logged printed in cleartext on stdout, though the run log, the report and --json masked it. The secret could come from vault.get or from a sensitive argument, and the method could log it through context.logger or console.*. Redaction had only ever been added to stderr and the run file sink.

This change covers three places that printed unmasked lines:

  • Method logger lines. wrapLoggerWithOutput masks each line with the run's redactor before the line becomes a method_output event. Every renderer, quiet-mode buffer and the serve event stream therefore gets it masked, as remote dispatches already did. The remote path keeps its own masking and passes no redactor, so nothing is masked twice.
  • Method console output. withConsoleGuard now installs in log mode too.
  • With no secret active, each call is forwarded unchanged.
  • Once a run has secrets, the call is rendered into one line, masked, and printed on the method's usual stream.
  • console.table, console.trace and console.assert join the captured set.
  • An Error, Map or Set is rendered with Deno.inspect, so it keeps its message, stack or entries rather than printing as {}.
  • Each patched method binds the stream
𝗗 release v20261105.023331.0-sha.f6de2ba3𝗖𝗟 f6de2ba3𝗣 #3625

Sswamp issue bug, feature and security can file against the platform source with --source platform (swamp-club#4239) (#3526)

Security-related change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-01T18:54:01Zissuefeat

What's Changed

  • feat(issue): swamp issue bug, feature and security can file against the platform source with --source platform (swamp-club#4239) (#3526)

Summary

Any logged-in user can now file a bug, feature or security report against the platform source with --source platform. Without the flag, the commands file with the source they use today.

  • --source accepts swamp or platform, and is refused with --extension and --email. The check runs before auth, extension lookup and the editor.
  • A report with a chosen source never falls back to email, which cannot carry a source. When not logged in it gets the login error with no prompt, and a timeout no longer suggests --email.
  • JSON output gains source: "platform" only for platform filings, and log output gains one line.
  • The platform source is separate from the admin-only platform type, which still cannot be filed from the CLI. The issue guide in the swamp skill says so.

Not in this PR

The manual page lives in the swamp-club repo, so it is not updated here.

Test Plan

  • Unit tests cover the validator, flag combinations, the request body, the libswamp pass-through, renderers, the not-logged-in refusal and the timeout message.
  • The compiled binary refuses each bad flag combination and value before the editor opens.
  • verify-build, verify-reviews and verify-skills passed on this commit.
  • No real issue was filed against the live API.

Athe TrustedHosts property test no longer generates reserved xn-- labels, which URL parsing correctly refuses (swamp-club#4627) (#3733)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-11T00:03:51Ztestfix

What's Changed

  • fix(test): the TrustedHosts property test no longer generates reserved xn-- labels, which URL parsing correctly refuses (swamp-club#4627) (#3733)

Summary

src/serve/local/trusted_hosts_property_test.ts failed for some fast-check seeds. Its label generator could produce a label such as xn--0: the ACE (punycode) prefix with an invalid encoding. URL parsing refuses that, so TrustedHosts.parse correctly rejected the entry and the property failed.

The generator now drops labels with hyphens in the third and fourth positions (the reserved R-LDH form, which includes every xn-- label). The parser is unchanged.

Fixes swamp-club#4627.

Test Plan

  • Reported counterexample (seed 1967052999, ["xn--0", undefined, 1, "a"]) reproduced before the change and passes after it
  • 2000 unseeded runs of the full property with the filtered generator: 0 failures (filter drops about 3.6% of labels)
  • deno test --repeats=50 on the file passes
  • verify-build, verify-reviews and verify-skills all green (attestation b5e83990-2aff-4511-a1ca-c240f9964344)

2026-11-10T22:28:35Zservefeat

What's Changed

  • feat(serve): swamp serve reload applies the access lists, audit, intervals and request-path settings without a restart (swamp-club#4602) (#3728)

Summary

Second half of the serve reload work (swamp-club#4602, part of #4439), after #3716 (swamp-club#4588). swamp serve reload (SIGHUP, the serve.reload WebSocket call and the config poller) now applies most of serve.yaml without a restart, and the seven review notes from #3716 are fixed.

How a reload runs (createServeSettingsReloader, src/serve/serve_settings_reloader.ts):

  • Prepare does everything that can fail: the strict re-read, startup's own checks, allowed-users lookups and the audit diff. A failure refuses the file and changes nothing.
  • Commit only assigns, through an applier per setting. The applier table is typed over the applied catalogue keys, so a reloadable setting without an applier fails the type check. The report lists only what commit applied; a partly applied setting shows under both applied and needs-restart.
  • Follow-ups do the async work: saving resolved names, swamp-club re-registration, starting queued webhook runs, rebuilding audit sinks. A failure is a warning that keeps the new values.
  • Each reload writes a serve.config.reloaded audit event under the policy in force before it, naming the caller and the settings, never a value.

  • Access lists (OAuth):
  • Removing a name from allowed-users always applies, at once.

Operator notice: serve now counts failed authentication attempts per principal and per client address on every transport. A lockout after five failures applies to that principal from that address only. The lockout warning in serve's log names the principal (a delegation, an integration or a minted token name) and the address, for example delegation:<id>|address:<ip>. Callers see the same 401 and 429 responses as before.

What changed

  • a delegated access, refresh or redeem secret: its delegation id
  • an integration credential: its integration id (integrationIdOfCredential, shared with the credential check)
  • any other name.secret token: its name
  • anything else: no principal, so the key is the address alone
  • Every failed-attempt site in serve uses it: the HTTP request and datastore routes, the WebSocket upgrade and peer-auth path, the signal and cancel routes, dashboard token and session logins, admin token and session auth, and the delegation routes' integration-credential check. The per-route key option on the HTTP caller is gone. Redeem and refresh keep their existing per-integration keys.

Athe swamp-club heartbeat names its instanceId, and a heartbeat answered 404 registers the process again (swamp-club#4587) (#3723)

`feat` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-10T19:52:26Zservefeat

What's Changed

  • feat(serve): the swamp-club heartbeat names its instanceId, and a heartbeat answered 404 registers the process again (swamp-club#4587) (#3723)

A serve process whose swamp-club registration is missing now registers itself again at its next hourly check-in, instead of staying off the collective Serve tab until it restarts.

Summary

Serves that share a datastore share one OAuth client, so swamp-club could not tell which process a heartbeat came from. The hourly swamp-club heartbeat now sends {"instanceId": "<id>"} as a JSON body. swamp-club, which lists one row per serve process, uses it to credit that process's own row.

  • A heartbeat answered 404 means swamp-club has no row for this process: its boot registration failed, or the row expired. The heartbeat service now registers again once, with the same request serve sent at boot. A registration that succeeds counts as that beat. One that fails is logged with swamp-club's reason and tried again at the next beat, so it never loops.
  • Any other heartbeat failure is logged as before and never registers again.
  • Stopping serve cancels any heartbeat still in flight.
  • Every process registers and heartbeats under its own instanceId, team-mode followers included, as before.

swamp-club already reads this body, and a swamp-club that ignores it is unaffected.

Test Plan

  • Unit tests for the heartbeat request (exact body and content-type; a 404 raises a distinct error, a 502 does not) and for the heartbeat

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-10T19:46:41Ztestfix

What's Changed

  • fix(test): the checkpoint-interval pinning test fires its mid-step checkpoints itself, so a slow report phase on a loaded host no longer adds a paced checkpoint (swamp-club#4605) (#3722)

Summary

team-mode workflow run: steps that outlast the checkpoint interval, one with a chunked output, have their requests to serve pinned failed once in verify-build under full-suite load. Two extra entries, a POST chunks/has and a POST partitions/{partition}/commits, appeared just before the reports' final batch.

Cause. The test ran with a real 500 ms checkpoint interval. Its slow steps waited for a paced checkpoint, but nothing kept the rest of the run under 500 ms. On a loaded host the summary-report phase outlasted the interval. The trailing timer armed after step two's flush then fired, and a paced checkpoint (its chunks/has and a lone partition commit) landed before the reports' batch. A temporary 700 ms stall before workflowReportRunner.runFor reproduces the CI diff exactly.

Fix (test-only, production pacing unchanged).

  • WorkflowRunCheckpointer gains firePacedCheckpointsForTesting(). It starts every armed trailing checkpoint now, inside the async context its save armed it in (AsyncLocalStorage.snapshot()). That matters because the checkpoint finds the run's open unit through that context, so it commits exactly what the timer would. It skips the push-back for a flush that landed meanwhile.
  • Every path that cancels a trailing checkpo

2026-11-10T19:36:31Zworkflowsfix

What's Changed

  • fix(workflows): a fan-out rollout that ends without reaching its canary gate holds the gate, so a retry is no longer refused over a pending gate (swamp-club#4599, #4595) (#3719)

Summary

A fan-out rollout could finish without ever reaching its canary gate, leaving the gate record pending in a finished job. A plain retry was then refused with Step "patch[approve-canary]" in job "patch" is pending; retry needs every step finished., together with a --from 'patch[approve-canary]' hint that --from does not accept. swamp-club#4599 (the gates property test failing with a fixed seed) and swamp-club#4595 report the same bug.

When every lane after the canary failed before it started (offline under a stored plan), runRolloutLanes counted those lanes as started, so the gate was never reached and the rollout ended complete. A gate never reached was only held on the halted end. Two paths reach this:

  • a --from retry that resets the gates, with no failure budget
  • a plain retry after a budget halt whose rerun halts with no lane left to hold, which runRolloutLanes also reports as complete

Changes:

  • JobRun.holdUnreachedFanOutGates() holds any still-pending gate record as a halt does (job_skipped, settledByAbort, no fanOutHalts entry), and the executor calls it when the rollout ends complete. A retry then asks the gate again.
  • The gates property test's model no longer copies the refusal (added in #3715 until this fix landed). It hold

Aa failed deno compile restores the version stamp and removes resources/deno before exiting (swamp-club#4600) (#3720)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-10T19:21:48Zbuildfix

What's Changed

  • fix(build): a failed deno compile restores the version stamp and removes resources/deno before exiting (swamp-club#4600) (#3720)

Summary

scripts/compile.ts called Deno.exit(1) inside its try when deno compile failed. Deno.exit ends the process at once, so the finally never ran: the stamped build info file stayed modified and the downloaded runtime stayed under resources/deno.

  • The build now returns its result out of the guarded section, and the entry point exits 1 only after main() has resolved, so restore always runs first.
  • runStampedBuild runs the stamp write and the build in try with restore in finally. Computing the stamp stays outside it: stampBuildInfo (from swamp-club#4591) is pure and throws before anything is written.
  • restoreBuildState rewrites src/infrastructure/runtime/build_info.ts only when a stamp was written, and removes resources/deno in its own finally, so a failed file write still removes the runtime. It replaces cleanupDenoResources.
  • The deno compile invocation moved into a compile() function; the argument list is unchanged (one conditional spread reflowed onto a single line by deno fmt).
  • AGENTS.md: the stamp is restored whether the build passes or fails; only an interrupted build (Ctrl-C or killed) can still leave it.

Rebased onto swamp-club#4591, which moved the stamped constants to build_info.ts and added scripts/compile_test.ts; both test sets are kept in that file.

Aswamp serve reload validates serve.yaml like startup, applies what it can and reports every changed setting, on every serve (swamp-club#4588) (#3716)

`feat` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-10T18:31:04Zservefeat

What's Changed

  • fix(datastore): setup extension resolves env and vault expressions in --config and persists them unresolved (swamp-club#4569) (#3717)

Summary

swamp datastore setup extension handed --config to the extension without

resolving ${{ env.* }} or ${{ vault.get() }}, although the load path

resolves them before schema validation and provider creation, as documented

under Config Value Interpolation. A datastore whose credential can only come

from config (for example @webframp/azure-blob-datastore with a service

principal) had no clean setup: a template failed backend auth, and the only

workaround wrote the literal secret into .swamp.yaml.

Fix

  • Setup resolves like load: datastoreSetupExtension resolves the config

through a new DatastoreSetupDeps.resolveConfigExpressions port before the

schema check. The resolved config drives validation, provider creation, the

format check, verify and migration. A resolution error (an unset variable, an

unknown vault) is a validation_failed error before any backend call or

config write.

  • Expressions are persisted, not values: .swamp.yaml receives the config

as given, so a vault secret never reaches disk.

  • CLI adapter: resolveSetupConfigExpressions in

src/cli/resolve_datastore.ts applies the load path's resolver, with the repo

marker's managedConfig gating vault expressions. Setup preserves that key,

so what resolves at setup resolves at the next load.

  • **Serve r
𝗗 release v20261110.182636.0-sha.7671fc36𝗖𝗟 7671fc36𝗣 #3717𝗣 #3716𝗔 lab#4569

Aflaky dashboard-session auth tests, multi-step lanes in the fan-out gates model test, and a halt that named a lane twice (swamp-club#4568, #4562, #4597) (#3715)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-10T18:18:44Ztestfix

What's Changed

  • fix(test): flaky dashboard-session auth tests, multi-step lanes in the fan-out gates model test, and a halt that named a lane twice (swamp-club#4568, #4562, #4597) (#3715)

Summary

swamp-club#4568: scheduled flaky-test job failing. In admin_auth_test.ts, authenticateDashboardSessionToken: refuses a person who is no longer admitted got 429 instead of 401 on its third repeat. A dashboard session's failure bucket is keyed on the session's token name, not the client address. So every test using the fixed session shared one bucket, and the sixth failure across repeats was rate-limited. Sweeping src/serve with --repeats 5 found the same fault in two more tests. All three now call resetRateLimitState() first, as AGENTS.md prescribes for tests that authenticate as a fixed principal.

swamp-club#4562: fan-out gates model test, lanes of several steps. integration/fleet_fan_out_gates_property_test.ts now generates lanes of one, two or three steps (prepare, apply, and verify under allowFailure). It adds an action that fails a single step part-way through a lane, and --from can now pick any lane step. The model tracks every lane record and states the retry rules independently of the executor:

  • A failed lane keeps the steps it finished before the retry's entry step.
  • A lane whose only failure is allowed is kept.
  • An offline lane fails all its unstarted steps.
  • --from a later step never asks an approved gate again.

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#4578, the follow-ups from #4567 (PR #3704). The issue's two team-mode ship blockers are fixed, the rest of its list is done, and team-mode read runs now lock (an architecture decision taken during planning).

Blocker 1: a serve-driven run and a member's run of one behavior collided on its method summary

Root cause:

  • acquireModelLocks committed a step's staged writes before releasing only when the lock was a member's ServeLock.
  • Serve's own runs hold the local createModelLock, which is the same lock serve leases to members. They released it with the step's versions still staged, and those versions committed later, at the run's next paced checkpoint or at its end.
  • A member granted the lease in that window gets its version floor from committed versions only. So it allocated the same version, committed first, and serve's commit was refused (version 1 is already live).

Fix: serve's step locks now commit before they release (commitBeforeRelease, the default in createStepLockHook). The CLI's hook opts out, so a solo CLI run is unchanged.

Blocker 2: a report lease lapse could fail a run whose steps succeeded

There were two causes:

1. One hook for steps and reports. The CLI's report lock was the step hook, whose `on

Docs changed: design/primitives/behaviors.md

  • datastore status and the lock listings opt out and answer at once.
  • Leader following, limited for token safety.
  • Only a leader on the configured serve's own host is followed, over https or loopback http.
  • A followed reply is used only when it names the same datastore log as the configured serve.

𝗗 release v20261110.163424.0-sha.a9942ff6𝗖𝗟 a9942ff6𝗣 #3711𝗔 lab#4579

Aa sender whose send fails keeps its lock for a linger, so a failing ingest no longer starts a sender per command (swamp-club#4580) (#3710)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-10T13:01:11Ztelemetryfix

What's Changed

  • fix(telemetry): a sender whose send fails keeps its lock for a linger, so a failing ingest no longer starts a sender per command (swamp-club#4580) (#3710)

Summary

Telemetry still added about 0.9 s to every command on the team-mode dogfood VMs after swamp-club#4511 (F33). This fixes the cause reproduced locally.

Cause

  • The detached telemetry sender from #4511 only lingers after a successful send.
  • When the ingest refuses, never answers, or returns a non-202 status, the sender exits after about 350 ms and frees sender.lock.
  • The spool still holds entries, so the next command claims the free lock and starts another sender: a full re-exec of the binary, about 480 ms of CPU. On a one-vCPU VM that competes with the following command.
  • A command with no distinct id hit the same loop: its sender exits at once without sending.

Fix

  • Failed send: the sender sends nothing more but keeps the lock for one linger (30 s), heartbeating it. The hold ends early if the lock is lost and stays within the 5-minute lifetime cap. A failing ingest now costs one sender per linger instead of one per command.
  • No distinct id: a command with neither a user id nor a repo id starts no sender.
  • Docs: design/operations.md describes the new exit rule.

Repro (linux arm64 container, one throttled core, 10 × swamp version, telemetry on vs off)

| Binary | Ingest | Senders started | On minus off |

|---|---|---|---|

| released `20261110.090527.

Docs changed: design/operations.md

Why it happened

  • a lock granted to a caller that had already given up blocked the next command for its 30 s TTL.

What changed

  • Member response deadline. Serve must start its reply within 10 s for reads, chunk checks and lock calls, plus the request's lock wait and upload allowance. Summary

A route without a leading slash, an unknown scheme, or a generic scheme without a header was installed, and the previous routes were dropped. The check runs for every route source. With --webhook flag routes, an invalid list in the file is now a reload error instead of the "list ignored" warning, which matches boot (boot refuses that file in flag mode too).

Trigger overrides have the same gap on reload and are tracked in swamp-club#4560.

Arefuse webhook routes whose extension scheme is a pulled type from an untrusted collective (swamp-club#4548) (#3696)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-09T20:15:46Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): refuse webhook routes whose extension scheme is a pulled type from an untrusted collective (swamp-club#4548) (#3696)

Summary

A webhook route naming an installed extension scheme used its handler with no collective trust check. The auto-resolver refuses to *pull* from an untrusted collective, but a type already installed (for example, pulled by hand) could verify, transform and respond on a live endpoint.

Serve now applies the collective trust list to every webhook route that uses an extension scheme:

  • Trust rule (evaluateWebhookTypeTrust, src/domain/webhooks/webhook_type_trust.ts):
  • local extension types (repo extension dirs, source mounts) are always trusted;
  • a pulled type needs both its scheme's collective and its owning extension's collective on trustedCollectives;
  • pulling or pinning confers no trust;
  • a type with no recorded origin is treated as pulled.
  • Origin: the extension loader records it from canonical, symlink-resolved paths (isPulledSourcePath) and passes it to the registry through RegistrationContext.origin.
  • Startup and reload:
  • every route source (serve.yaml, --webhook, route files) goes through the same check;
  • the error names the route, scheme and collective, and gives the swamp extension trust add fix;
  • a refused route file is refused on its own, like any other bad file.
  • Every delivery:
  • the trust check reads the same registry entry the handler is built
Docs changed: .claude/skills/swamp/references/extension/references/webhook/api.md

Summary

Each webhook route file is parsed on its own, on every startup and every

still reported success.

(src/serve/webhook.ts) marks a failure about the environment, not the file:

  • a configured vault that can't answer
  • vaults that can't be opened
  • a webhook type lookup that throws

unconfigured vault, an empty value, and an uninstalled scheme. This follows the

same rule checkSecretReference uses.

  • Keeping the live route. On a reload, a file that fails this way keeps its

live endpoint, with the secret resolved when it last loaded. This only happens

when the same file holds the same route with an unchanged entry. The check

compares values directly, so key order doesn't count and YAML timestamps and

binary values are compared exactly. The reload lists each kept route among its

errors as Kept the live endpoint for webhook route file ….

Alarge team-mode outputs upload under a size-scaled deadline, serve stores chunks while reading, failing uploads are capped at 3, and a lapse mid-request reports lease_lapsed (swamp-club#4542) (#3694)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-09T19:16:29Zdatastorefix

What's Changed

  • fix(datastore): large team-mode outputs upload under a size-scaled deadline, serve stores chunks while reading, failing uploads are capped at 3, and a lapse mid-request reports lease_lapsed (swamp-club#4542) (#3694)

Summary

swamp-club#4542 (F35 from the #4374 dogfood): a team member writing a 64 MiB output through serve took minutes and usually lost it, reported as serve being unreachable. A local reproduction measured four causes, fixed here:

  • Size-scaled request deadline. A serve request's deadline is the flat timeout plus its body at 256 KiB/s, so a large commit is no longer cut off at 30 s on a slow link. Bodiless requests keep the flat timeout.
  • Serve stores carried chunks while reading the next, at most 4 per request, under a 128 MiB serve-wide budget for chunks read and waiting on storage (taken after a chunk is read, so a slow sender holds none of it). A failed store fails the request with nothing recorded in the upload ledger, and every store settles before serve answers.
  • Failing uploads are capped. A chunk-carrying commit that serve answers with a 5xx is uploaded at most 3 times per process; later flushes fail it without sending and the outbox keeps it. Before, every later flush of the command re-uploaded it (9 times in the reproduction).
  • A lapse mid-request reports datastore_v3_lease_lapsed, naming the lease and the upload, instead of datastore_v3_serve_unreachable. Exit codes are unchanged (1 once something land

What's Changed

  • feat(swamp-lib): link a person, act over HTTP and refresh delegated tokens; a delegated refresh keeps the refresh token (swamp-club#4543) (#3686)

Operator notice

  • A delegated refresh no longer rotates the refresh token. POST /api/v1/delegations/refresh mints a new 1-hour access token and keeps the refresh token, so two copies of an app refreshing at once, or a lost reply, can no longer end a link. Serve accepts the current access token and the two before it, each until it expires. Serve no longer detects a reused refresh token: every refresh already needs the integration credential, and this trade-off goes to the pre-pilot security review.
  • The refresh reply changed: status is refreshed, with no refreshToken. A refused refresh answers 401 invalid_grant with a reason: mismatch, unknown, not_issued, not_admitted, ended, expired, superseded or unreadable. Only not_admitted recovers by itself, once the person is admitted again.
  • Upgrade every serve node. Links, redeem and refresh now need every live node to advertise delegation-links-2. Until then they answer team_not_ready and nothing changes. Access tokens already issued keep working for up to an hour.

swamp-lib

A new @swamp-club/swamp-lib version adds HTTP clients for an integration acting for a person. Deno only. They refuse to run in a browser because they hold bearer credentials.

  • SwampIntegrationClient: startLink, redeemLink, `refresh

Aremoving a person from allowed-users or allowed-collectives refuses their tokens, integration links and open sessions (swamp-club#4530) (#3685)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-09T14:06:18Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): removing a person from allowed-users or allowed-collectives refuses their tokens, integration links and open sessions (swamp-club#4530) (#3685)

Summary

Admission (auth.allowed-users, auth.allowed-collectives) was only checked when a person signed in with device login or consented to an integration link. Removing someone from either list — even across a restart — or their leaving an allowed collective upstream left their server tokens, delegated tokens and open sessions working until the tokens expired (30 days for a login token). The design docs claimed otherwise.

Admission is now checked wherever a person's authority is accepted, against the lists in force and the credential's recorded collectives:

  • Presented credentials — a login-issued server token (one with an OAuth identity, or an older one with a stored OAuth access token), a delegated access token or a dashboard session is refused with reason not-admitted and audited as auth.token.not_admitted. A refused dashboard session is not invalidated.
  • Delegated refresh — refused with not_admitted without ending the link.
  • Runs acting later — auto-resume (decideSubjectAccess, both branches, and the resume launcher's delegation guard) and run vault access (token- and delegation-bound) refuse a person who is no longer admitted, as they do for a revoked token.
  • Open sessions — the revalidation pass closes them with code 4003, cause not-admitted, within 30 s.
𝗗 release v20261109.140303.0-sha.ecf6c21b𝗖𝗟 ecf6c21b𝗣 #3685𝗣 #3683

Afan-out rollout: lanes start in canary batches under an error budget, hold the rest on a halt, and resume continues the rollout (swamp-club#4531) (#3683)

`feat` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-09T10:38:27Zworkflowsfeat

What's Changed

  • feat(workflows): fan-out rollout: lanes start in canary batches under an error budget, hold the rest on a halt, and resume continues the rollout (swamp-club#4531) (#3683)

Summary

A fanOut job that sets rollout now runs instead of being refused (swamp-club#4531).

  • Batches settle. Lanes start batch by batch from the frozen plan. nextBatch is consulted before every lane start, and the job's concurrency caps lanes inside a batch.
  • Error budget. Once failed lanes exceed the plan-time maxFailures (a count, or a percentage rounded down), no lane starts. Running lanes finish, and lanes that never started are held: their pending steps are skipped as job_skipped and settledByAbort. The job fails, and downstream succeeded jobs are skipped.
  • Halt record. JobRun.haltFanOut holds the lanes, appends to fanOutHalts and fails the job in one call. The run-record judge (fanOutHaltRefusal) accepts an entry only in the record where its job turns failed, never lets it change or be dropped, and checks it against the plan and the lane records.
  • Resume continues the rollout with a fresh budget. The lanes the last halt failed run first, then the other re-run lanes in the frozen batches (resumeRollout). Passed lanes are never re-sent.
  • Output. New job_fanout_batch_started and job_fanout_halted events, plus batches, budget and held fields, in log and JSON modes, for rollout jobs only. Serve sends the new kinds only to client

𝗗 release v20261109.003948.0-sha.b1781b00𝗖𝗟 b1781b00𝗣 #3679𝗔 lab#4438

Averify-build runs the test suite in balanced parallel processes, about a quarter faster with every test still run (swamp-club#4505) (#3678)

`feat` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-09T00:19:15Ztestingfeat

What's Changed

  • feat(testing): verify-build runs the test suite in balanced parallel processes, about a quarter faster with every test still run (swamp-club#4505) (#3678)

Nothing changes in the swamp CLI. This changes only how verify-build runs swamp's own test suite.

What this changes

verify-build's test step now runs the suite through a new deno task test-shards, which splits it into several smaller deno test processes and reports one combined result. In one large process the test files contend on process-wide locks, so smaller processes finish sooner and use less CPU. Every test still runs and none changes.

  • Balanced shards. One shard per 8 cores, up to 4. Files are assigned slowest first, using the recorded test time in scripts/test_shard_weights.json. A file it doesn't list counts as fast, so stale weights only make the balance worse; they never drop a test. --record-weights refreshes them.
  • One result. Each shard's output streams live with a [n] prefix. At the end there is one summary with every shard's counts and the ERRORS and FAILURES blocks of any shard that failed. The run fails if any shard fails or leaves temp entries behind.
  • Same flags. Shards read their flags and environment from the test task in deno.json, so the two can't drift.
  • Short temp paths. Each shard's TMPDIR is a one-character directory under the caller's TMPDIR. Under verify-build's TMPDIR, Litestream's socket tests have only a few characters to spare
𝗗 release v20261109.001436.0-sha.04e1cc33𝗖𝗟 04e1cc33𝗣 #3678𝗔 lab#4505

AworkflowRunStream and modelMethodRunStream throw a refusal or failure instead of ending as if nothing went wrong (swamp-club#4445) (#3677)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-09T00:11:14Zswamp-libfix

What's Changed

  • fix(swamp-lib): workflowRunStream and modelMethodRunStream throw a refusal or failure instead of ending as if nothing went wrong (swamp-club#4445) (#3677)

> Behaviour change for swamp-lib callers. workflowRunStream and modelMethodRunStream now throw when a run is refused, fails, or loses its socket. They used to be able to end as if the run had finished cleanly. Every event that arrived is still delivered before the throw. This ships as the next 0.<date>.<N> release of @swamp-club/swamp-lib.

Summary

The stream queue settled a waiting reader with "done" when an error arrived. So a refusal sent while the consumer was waiting ended the for await loop with no events and no exception, and a refused run read as a clean finish (swamp-club#4445). The same path dropped:

  • a socket that closed mid-run;
  • events already buffered when an error arrived, including the in-band error event that ordinary run failures send. Whether a consumer saw that event depended on timing.

The queue now lives in packages/client/queue.ts, which mod.ts doesn't export, and has one terminal state:

  • whichever of done and error comes first wins;
  • buffered events are delivered before the error;
  • an error rejects a waiting reader;
  • an error with no reader is only stored, so it can't cause an unhandled rejection.

Both stream helpers end an in-band error event through the same reject path. A refused or failed stream therefore throws the same SwampClientError (

What's Changed

  • adds a snapshot to a job whose plan is already written
  • holds a snapshot without a plan, including in a run's first record

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-08T22:55:20Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): a serve restarted after kill -9 settles the run it left running at boot (swamp-club#4518) (#3675)

Summary

  • Bug: after kill -9 of a native swamp serve mid-run, a serve restarted within 90s of the run's start skipped the killed run at boot ("tracker reports still running"), so its record stayed running until a CLI workflow recover settled it. Each serve boot mints a new instance id, and the run tracker judged pid locality by instance id, so the restarted serve treated its own previous instance's row as foreign: the dead-process reap skipped it and the TTL reap had not yet fired.
  • Fix: run-tracker rows record the owning process's host identity (hostname plus pid namespace; schema v7, host_identity column). The dead-process reap also takes a row whose host identity matches this process's, so the boot reaper interrupts the run with server_crash. The TTL reap and ActiveRun.isLocalTo are unchanged, so no reap that happens today is held back.
  • Safety: a row is stamped only when it names this host, and on Linux only when the pid namespace can be read; a bare hostname is shared by sibling containers. Rows from another pid namespace, and rows written before the column, keep the instance-id rule. A live peer serve in the same namespace is protected by the pid check.
  • Design doc design/enablers/run-tracker.md updated.

Test plan

  • [x] Unit tests: earlier instance in this namespace reaped; another namespace and no id

Recover

  • The interrupt rules are unchanged. They only touch pending steps, so a finished lane keeps its result.
  • CLI recover prints the resume command with --server <url> for fan-out workflows.

  • JobRun.fanOutSnapshot sits beside the frozen plan. It holds the authored selec

𝗗 release v20261108.025705.0-sha.f4852124𝗖𝗟 f4852124𝗣 #3671𝗣 #4506𝗔 lab#4516

Aa slow or silent OTLP collector no longer exhausts file descriptors or holds a command's exit, and a burst of commands starts one telemetry sender (swamp-club#4511) (#3670)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-08T02:25:19Zobservabilityfix

What's Changed

  • fix(observability): a slow or silent OTLP collector no longer exhausts file descriptors or holds a command's exit, and a burst of commands starts one telemetry sender (swamp-club#4511) (#3670)

Summary

Observability no longer slows or breaks the command it observes (swamp-club#4511, group 5 of the #4374 dogfood fixes). This fixes F36 and F33 from dogfood run 4.

F36: the span and log exporters could exhaust file descriptors

  • Cause: the OTLP span and log exporters started one fetch for every span or record, with no limit on how many were in flight. Against a slow collector, sockets piled up until the command's own file I/O failed with os error 24.
  • The fix: both exporters now send through OtlpExportQueue:
  • one request in flight at a time; anything that ends meanwhile rides the next request, up to 512 items;
  • past 2,048 queued items, new ones are dropped and counted in swamp.export.spans under a new dropped outcome;
  • every span counts as attempted when it is queued, so dropped spans stay in the count.
  • Exit: shutdown waits on all signals for at most EXIT_DRAIN_MS (1 s) in total, not each in-flight send's 10 s timeout.
  • Logs and spans drain at the same time; metrics collects last, within the same deadline, so the final metrics export includes the last span outcomes.
  • forceFlush keeps waiting for in-flight sends; each send still gives up after 10 s.
  • Not a regression: this was triaged as a plain bug.
Docs changed: design/enablers/remote-execution-telemetry.md

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#4514. The issue lifecycle ran the code conformance review before verification, so code changed during the verification loop reached a PR without ever being compared to the plan. The governance order is now:

plan → adversarial review → approve → implement → verify (fix until it passes) → verification_passed → code conformance review of the verified commit → justify deviations → checklist → post_attestation → link_pr

The conformance review records deviations and never changes code. Changing code instead of justifying a deviation is a new commit, which goes back through verification_failed and is verified and reviewed again.

Model (extensions/models/issue_lifecycle.ts, _lib/schemas.ts), version 2026.11.08.1

  • verify records the commit under verification on state. verification_passed refuses a different commit.
  • code_conformance_review runs only in verifying. It needs a passing result for the commit verify started on, and records that commit on the review. verifiedCommit is the single definition of that rule, shared by the method and the gate.
  • code-conformance-clear gates post_attestation, link_pr and complete. In verifying and pr_failed it requires a justified review of the verified commit. pr_open and `relea

Aa step dispatched to a worker that has just enrolled waits for enrollment to finish instead of failing with an unknown method (swamp-club#4504) (#3667)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-08T01:08:26Zworkerfix

What's Changed

  • fix(worker): a step dispatched to a worker that has just enrolled waits for enrollment to finish instead of failing with an unknown method (swamp-club#4504) (#3667)

A step sent to a worker that has just enrolled no longer fails with "No handler registered for method 'worker.dispatch'".

What this fixes

Serve lists a newly enrolled worker as idle, and wakes the queued steps waiting for one, before it sends the enroll reply. The worker set up its dispatch handler only after that reply arrived. A step dispatched in that gap got unknown_method, which fails the step instead of re-queuing it. The worker has always done it in this order, so this isn't a regression. It mostly shows up under load, for example as an occasional failure of the remote execution "elastic queueing" test when the suite runs in shards.

What changes

  • The worker sets up its dispatch handler before it asks to enroll, and holds any dispatch that arrives early until enrollment, the gate check and the worker's own setup are complete.
  • If enrollment is refused, the gate refuses the worker, or the connection drops first, the held dispatch is never run and serve sees the connection drop.

No protocol change. Serve is unchanged, so workers on an older release keep the race until they upgrade.

Test Plan

  • Three new tests where a fake serve dispatches before its enroll reply: the dispatch waits and runs only after enrollment, and with a refused enrollment or a gate refusal it never
𝗗 release v20261108.010329.0-sha.05af4aa3𝗖𝗟 05af4aa3𝗣 #3667𝗔 lab#4504

Aa fan-out job runs one pinned lane per matching worker from serve's worker pool, and its plan is frozen in the run record (swamp-club#4506) (#3666)

`feat` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-08T01:02:20Zworkflowsfeat

What's Changed

  • feat(workflows): a fan-out job runs one pinned lane per matching worker from serve's worker pool, and its plan is frozen in the run record (swamp-club#4506) (#3666)

Summary

A job with fanOut now runs once per matching worker, as a lane pinned to that worker (swamp-club#4506, Phase 2 of the fleet fan-out work).

What happens at job start

  • The job reads serve's worker pool through a module-level WorkerPoolReader, so nested runs reach it too.
  • It plans the lanes, records the plan, then saves the run before any lane starts.
  • Lane steps are ordinary steps of the job, named step[worker]. The step-start journal, interrupt marking, the v3 run-record judge and history therefore treat them like any other step.

How a lane runs

  • Each lane walks the job's step DAG on its own and is pinned to its planned worker by a fixed target.
  • dependsOn, guards and cleanup read only that lane's steps. steps.<name> inside a lane reads the same lane's step.
  • self.worker (name, labels, platform, arch) is in scope.
  • behaviorName gets a -<worker> suffix unless its authored text is built from self.worker.name. A plan in which two workers would share one instance is refused.

Worker eligibility

  • An unverified or draining worker fails its lane with worker_ineligible, and nothing is sent.
  • An offline member waits until its worker reconnects, including as a restarted process, or until queueTimeout.
  • offline: skip and offline: fail

Aa run that writes thousands of resources no longer fails with Invalid string length, and keeps at most 16 MiB of resource attributes (swamp-club#4469) (#3652)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-06T20:09:55Zworkflowsfix

What's Changed

  • fix(workflows): a run that writes thousands of resources no longer fails with Invalid string length, and keeps at most 16 MiB of resource attributes (swamp-club#4469) (#3652)

Summary

A method run or workflow run that writes thousands of resources succeeded, then failed while printing its result with Invalid string length. Every resource's attributes were held in memory and embedded in the --json result, which passed V8's string limit. This PR caps the attributes a run carries. Content past the cap is still written and can be read with swamp data get.

The budget. A run keeps at most 16 MiB of resource attributes, counted in stored bytes (ResourceAttributeBudget). The cap applies when the run writes its resources and again when its results are read back. Once a run passes it:

  • a resource handle carries no attributes;
  • the run's result lists the artifact with attributesOmitted: true instead of its content;
  • a step whose merged outputs left any resource out carries outputsPartial: true.

The budget only ever omits a run's last resources. A recorded size is checked before the read, so content past the budget is never loaded.

Where it applies:

  • swamp behavior run (--json, and the log-mode Data produced box, which gives one retrieval hint for all omitted artifacts)
  • swamp workflow run and swamp workflow resume
  • swamp workflow history get, and serve's workflow.history.get
  • A nested workflow step counts only its
Docs changed: .claude/skills/swamp/references/extension/references/model/api.md

What's Changed

  • feat(serve): grant conditions read the client and a run's initiator, so an operator can forbid self-approval and refuse an integration (swamp-club#4473) (#3650)

Summary

Grant conditions gain two fields, so separation of duties is something an operator writes as a grant.

  • client, on every resource kind, holds the token's <kind>:<id>. A token with no client reads as direct:.
  • run, on workflow grants for approve only (which also covers reject), holds run.initiator.kind, .id, .sub and run.serveDriven.

To forbid self-approval, write a deny, once per subject that holds run, approve or admin:

```

deny approve workflow:* when principal.sub == run.initiator.sub

```

An allow isn't enough, because run implies approve and admins fall back to allowed.

Rules:

  • A run with no usable initiator has no run, so a deny refuses and an allow doesn't match. A service run has an empty sub, so people can approve it and no user id can collide with it.
  • Approve and reject decide twice: before any run is read, so a person with no grant gets the same reply whether or not the run exists; then on the record read under the run's claim, before any write.
  • Client conditions never narrow a run, because run delegates use of what it touches. A vault deny on client stops direct requests, and deny run keeps an integration out of a workflow.
  • Grants that don't name the new fields decide as before.
  • Writing a grant refuses `run

𝗗 release v20261106.161124.0-sha.7a206413𝗖𝗟 7a206413𝗣 #3646𝗔 lab#4447

Athe run summary box keeps its right border aligned when a value is cut, a header is long or a value holds wide characters (swamp-club#4471) (#3645)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-06T15:52:06Zoutputfix

What's Changed

  • fix(output): the run summary box keeps its right border aligned when a value is cut, a header is long or a value holds wide characters (swamp-club#4471) (#3645)

Summary

The Arguments / Data produced box printed after swamp behavior run and swamp workflow run drew its right border in the wrong column. The box was capped at 80 columns, but each value was cut to a fixed 60 characters regardless of the key column, so any row under a key longer than 12 characters overflowed. Artifact headers (<name> from <source>) were never cut, and widths were counted in UTF-16 code units, so CJK text and emoji shifted the border too.

  • Every row is built as plain-text segments, fitted to the 80-column limit by display width (unicodeWidth from @std/cli, already a dependency), and painted afterwards, so a cut never splits a color code. This follows fitSegments in update_check.ts.
  • A value gets the columns its row has left instead of a fixed 60, so short keys show more of their value.
  • The key column pads by display width.
  • Color codes inside data values (for example a captured colored stdout) are stripped, so they are measured and shown as text.
  • A key wider than the box is cut with an ellipsis, and that row shows no value.
  • The two key-width sites use maxOf instead of spreading into Math.max; their pinned entries in spread_call_rules_test.ts are removed.

Only console_writer.ts changes; neither caller does. JSON mode is unaffected.

𝗗 release v20261106.154649.0-sha.c33d6c78𝗖𝗟 c33d6c78𝗣 #3645𝗔 lab#4471

Aa large parallel forEach finishes within a second of its last step instead of minutes later (swamp-club#4470) (#3644)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-06T15:42:48Zworkflowsfix

What's Changed

  • fix(workflows): a large parallel forEach finishes within a second of its last step instead of minutes later (swamp-club#4470) (#3644)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#4470. A job with a large parallel forEach stayed open long after its last step finished. The delay grew with the square of the step count: 66s after the last step at 200 iterations, and about 7 minutes at 500.

Cause. The run loop saved the whole run record once for every step_started, step_completed, step_failed and step_skipped event.

  • merge and mergeGated drain step streams eagerly, so every iteration finishes in about a second and 2N events pile up behind the loop.
  • Each save re-reads and rewrites a record that grows with N (about 20 KB per behavior step), so the queue costs O(N²).
  • The report said nothing was written during the gap. That was a modification-time artifact: polling the run YAML showed it rewritten about 4 times a second through the whole gap, always the same ~2 MB of final state.

Fix. Skip a save that a finished save already covers.

  • New RunSaveHorizon (src/domain/workflows/run_save_horizon.ts): each step event gets a sequence number in runStep when it is produced.
  • saveRun notes the latest number when a save starts. Once the write and checkpoint succeed, every event up to that number counts as covered.
  • Both run loops (fresh run and resume) skip the per-event save for a covered event. Events without a number, and runs rebuilt from data, nev

What's Changed

So every nested-name package (@swamp/gcp/*, @swamp/aws/*) was attributed to a parent with no manifest, and runModel authorization failed closed. A dependency on a nested extension such as @swamp/aws/ec2 could never match on the target side either.

The owner of a pulled source is now resolved in three steps:

1. Lockfile: the deepest path prefix that is an installed extension, via a new ExtensionRepository.isInstalledExtension. No manifest is read.

2. Without lockfile data: the outermost manifest.yaml decides. It owns the source only if it is a regular file whose name equals its own directory; otherwise the search stops there. A package can ship a manifest.yaml naming one of its own subdirectories. This rule keeps such a manifest from giving that code dependencies the published manifest never declared.

3. Otherwise: the old two-segment guess. A pulled source is never unnamed, because an unnamed ca

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-05T20:37:29Ztestfix

What's Changed

  • fix(test): the two-step workflow-run pinned-requests test no longer flakes when the run outlasts the 2 s checkpoint interval on a slow host (swamp-club#4441) (#3633)

Summary

team-mode workflow run: a two-step run's requests to serve are pinned failed once in verify-build. It pins the exact order of a team member's requests to serve.

The issue said the failure was an extra commits read. The request counts matched, though. One run-record commit went out before step two's POST locks, and its read-back came after the lock's catch-up.

Cause. WorkflowRunCheckpointer spaces checkpoints by a real 2 s interval.

  • Step one's start save arms a trailing checkpoint about 2 s out.
  • A fast run takes about 130 ms, and its end save clears that timer.
  • On a loaded host the run outlasted the interval, so the timer fired mid-run.
  • The run does not wait for that checkpoint, so its commit raced step two's lock.

Forcing the timer to fire at step two's start save reproduces the CI diff line for line.

Fix (test-only, production unchanged).

  • WorkflowRunCheckpointer reads its default interval from a module-level value that setRunCheckpointIntervalForTesting sets. An explicit intervalMs still wins, and every production construction site keeps 2 s.
  • The two-step test sets the interval to one hour for its body, so the trailing checkpoint never fires mid-run. The flattened pinned list is unchanged.
  • The test's step comments now match the order observe

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-05T19:16:07Zworkflowsfix

What's Changed

  • fix(workflows): a cancelled run no longer starts a step queued on its model lock, keeps an in-flight method's lock, and lets a data write already under way land before it settles or a second Ctrl+C exits (swamp-club#4434) (#3632)

Summary

A Ctrl+C during a fanned-out run could leave an external side effect with no persisted record (swamp-club#4434). Four things combined:

  • steps queued on a sibling's model lock still started their method after the abort;
  • the datastore sync coordinator's first-SIGINT handler released every model lock, even for commands that own their shutdown;
  • the run abandoned in-flight work after the 4 s step stop grace, and the CLI exited while a write was under way;
  • a second Ctrl+C exited at once.

The report's suspicion that the abort severs the local write RPC did not hold: local writes take no signal, and serve persists a remote write it has accepted. The loss came from process exit.

Changes

  • No new work after the cancel. executeModelMethod checks the step's signal after taking its lock (inside the try, so the lock is still released). The lock wait itself stops on the signal: StepLockHook → createStepLockHook → acquireModelLocks → registerDatastoreSyncNamed → an optional signal on DistributedLock.acquire, honoured by FileLock and ServeLock between attempts only, so a lease serve granted is never orphaned. Cleanup steps run under their own cleanup signal and are unaffected.
  • **Locks held on

2026-11-05T15:03:39Zreleasefeat

What's Changed

  • feat(release): the compiled binary no longer embeds about 110 MB of unused npm packages, and a per-target size limit fails the build on a jump (swamp-club#4424) (#3629)

Fixes swamp-club#4424.

The compiled binary embedded every npm package Deno resolved, including the dashboard's build tools, although scripts/compile.ts already embeds the built dist. This change embeds only npm packages reachable from main.ts and adds a size limit, so they cannot return unnoticed.

Changes

  • scripts/compile.ts passes --exclude-unused-npm. The workspace, deno.lock resolution of retained packages and dashboard tooling do not change.
  • The root @opentelemetry/semantic-conventions import is pinned to 1.28.0, the version the SDK packages require, which removes a duplicate. The pin has to move with any later SDK upgrade.
  • scripts/check_binary_size.ts checks binaries against per-target limits in scripts/binary_size_limits.json. verify-build checks the host target; release.yml checks all five before anything uploads. An intended increase raises the limit in the same change.

Sizes

Real scripts/compile.ts builds with the real dist and embedded deno:

| Target | Before | After | Limit |

|---|---|---|---|

| Linux x64 | 402.1 MB | 291.6 MB | 307 MB |

| Linux arm64 | 388.3 MB | 282.0 MB | 297 MB |

| macOS x64 | 365.1 MB | 269.9 MB | 285 MB |

| macOS arm64 | 335.5 MB | 241.6 MB | 257 MB |

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-04T23:20:52Zpersistencefix

What's Changed

  • fix(persistence): on Windows, clearing the output summaries no longer warns when another clear or a reader holds what it moves (swamp-club#4397) (#3622)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#4397. On Windows, removeStaleOutputSummaries logged a false "Clearing the output summaries … failed" warning, and output_summary_index_test.ts "clears racing each other" failed intermittently (12 of 29 Windows Compatibility runs).

Causes

I reproduced both on a Windows VM, on the first run.

  • Clears racing each other. Windows opens a rename's source by name and then renames the handle. A losing clear that opened v1 before the winner moved it follows the directory to its new .cleared-* name. Once the winner starts deleting it, the loser gets os error 5 (PermissionDenied), not NotFound.
  • A reader holding a shard open. Windows won't rename a directory while any file inside it is open, for reading or for appending. So a clear that overlaps a history read or append hit the same error.
  • The cleared marker. With Defender's real-time scanning on, a newly written marker is sometimes held for hundreds of milliseconds: one refusal lasted 272ms. Every other clear's rename onto it is refused while that lasts.

Changes

All in src/infrastructure/persistence/output_summary_index.ts:

  • Moving an entry aside: a refused move is retried once, after a short pause.
  • NotFound: another clear took the entry, so it's skipped.
  • The retry succeeds: the e

Awatch_pr watches the linked PR, records a conflict or failed check as pr_failed, and closes a merged issue out to done (swamp-club#4408) (#3621)

`feat` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-04T23:04:07Zissue-lifecyclefeat

What's Changed

  • feat(issue-lifecycle): watch_pr watches the linked PR, records a conflict or failed check as pr_failed, and closes a merged issue out to done (swamp-club#4408) (#3621)

Summary

After post_attestation and link_pr, the issue lifecycle stopped at pr_open. Nothing watched the PR, so an auto-merged PR left the swamp-club issue in_progress, and a PR with a merge conflict sat there with nobody told.

watch_pr is a new method on @swamp/issue-lifecycle, run from pr_open. It reads the PR through gh until it reaches a final state or hits its time limit (30 minutes by default), then moves the lifecycle on by calling the existing methods through context.runModel. That way the existing checks (valid-transition, pr-cooldown) and the swamp-club records are reused rather than copied.

  • Merged: runs pr_merged (sets the issue to shipped), then notify, then summarize (done). The summary inputs are required up front, and linked-issue outcomes are checked before the first read.
  • Merge conflict, failed check, changes requested, or closed without merging: runs pr_failed with the reason. A conflict is logged on the first read. If a final state shows up inside the 3-minute cooldown, watch_pr waits out the window and reads the PR again before acting.
  • A hold label (configurable) or the time limit: ends the watch with no transition.
  • Every case: writes a new prWatch record. If a nested step fails, it records that step a
Docs changed: .claude/skills/github-pr/SKILL.md

Aon Windows, extension_assets_dispatch_test compares the shipped filesRoot with the models directory in canonical form, as the catalog stores it (swamp-club#4399) (#3620)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-04T22:45:55Ztestfix

What's Changed

  • fix(test): on Windows, extension_assets_dispatch_test compares the shipped filesRoot with the models directory in canonical form, as the catalog stores it (swamp-club#4399) (#3620)

Summary

integration/extension_assets_dispatch_test.ts has failed on every Windows Compatibility run since it landed with swamp-club#4282. A lazily loaded local extension takes its source path from the extension catalog, which keys paths by canonicalizePath: lower-cased with forward slashes on Windows, by design. The bundle's filesRoot inherits that form, so asserting it equal to the native temp path could only pass on POSIX.

The assertion now compares canonicalizePath of both sides. That is the identity on POSIX, so the check is unchanged there. assertPathEquals would not have been enough: it swaps separators but does not fold case. No source change: every consumer of filesRoot (bundle hashing, data plane asset serving, worker exec) derives root and file paths from that one value, which works on case-insensitive NTFS.

Fixes swamp-club#4399.

  • Reproduced on a Windows 11 VM (Deno 2.9.8) at dd6591148: the test failed at the filesRoot assertion with c:/users/docker/appdata/... against C:\Users\Docker\AppData\....
  • With this change, the same test passes on the Windows VM and on Linux, including the asset-hash checks that read the files through that root.
  • Checked the CI log of run 41864132261: both sides carry the runner's RUNNER~1 short

What's Changed

  • findAllGlobalSince: skips a file named before the cutoff without a stat or read, however fresh its mtime
  • findHistoryByDefinition: with since, skips a file named before it without a stat or read, and keeps one started exactly at it

Cause. withTempDir turns the summary index off by writing a plain file at .swamp/_output_summaries. An index append through that file fails differently on each OS:

| Call through a file | Linux | Windows |

| --- | --- | --- |

| append | NotADirectory | NotFound |

| stat | NotADirectory | NotFound |

| mkdir -p | NotADirectory | AlreadyExists |

OutputSummaryIndex.#appendLines falls back to ensureDir only on NotFound. So on Windows the read's backfill stats the shard directory twice: once before the mkdir, and once after it returns AlreadyExists. countFileOps counted those stats.

Fix. countFileOps now leaves out any path with an _output_summaries segment. The tests still assert that the record skipped by name is never stat'ed or read. The index's own I/O is covered by integration/output_summary_index_test.ts.

  • Writes through serve's datastore API. DatastoreApi.#afterCommit no longer invalidates. It only runs on v3.

  • A global-lock open of a namespaced v3 repository (data gc, datastore compact, model validate, …). repo_context.ts skips the namespace invalidate on v3. Found during verification.
  • Restore. A reader whose restore moved the commits under it replays through V3Datastore.replayEveryCommit. That writes a replay-pending marker to the read-model state before any await, so a killed process still replays, and the next lock section resets as the read model's own reset.

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-04T19:39:52Zdoctorfix

What's Changed

  • fix(doctor): doctor datastores --repair closes the catalog after its duplicate-latest stage, so on Windows the later catalog removal no longer fails with os error 32 (swamp-club#4395) (#3612)

Summary

On Windows, swamp doctor datastores --repair -y failed at step 5/5 of contamination repair with os error 32, and all six tests in integration/doctor_repair_unpushed_cache_files_test.ts failed on every Windows Compatibility run.

The file Windows refused to remove was .swamp/data/_catalog.db, not the namespace cache that the issue named. Step 5 is catalog invalidation (removeCatalogDb). The duplicate-latest repair stage (createDuplicateLatestRepairDeps) opened a CatalogStore on that file when its deps were built and never closed it, so the handle stayed open for the rest of the run. POSIX lets a process remove a file it still has open; Windows does not.

Each dep now opens the store, makes its one call and closes it in a finally. That matches the other catalog store sites in the command, including the unreadable-versions stage, which already closes its store so that a later stage can remove the file. The leak dates from swamp-club#1902. swamp-club#4138 added the first tests that run a confirmed repair through step 5 on Windows, which is when it showed up.

𝗗 release v20261104.193314.0-sha.b24d9e09𝗖𝗟 b24d9e09𝗣 #3612𝗔 lab#4395

Aa serve given its own OAuth client id and secret leaves flat oauth-* secrets in a shared user vault alone, neither migrating nor reading them (swamp-club#4265) (#3611)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-04T18:49:44Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): a serve given its own OAuth client id and secret leaves flat oauth-* secrets in a shared user vault alone, neither migrating nor reading them (swamp-club#4265) (#3611)

Summary

On a first start, serve's OAuth migration lists the default user vault and moves any oauth-* secrets it finds into _token-secrets, deleting them from the vault. A vault such as AWS Secrets Manager is flat across an account and region, so those secrets may belong to another instance.

A start with a supplied client id and secret now leaves them alone:

  • migrateOAuthSecrets returns before any vault access and writes no marker.
  • Credential resolution does not fall back to the user vault. With no marker to close that fallback, write access to the vault could choose admins on every start.
  • Admins are resolved afresh, which needs a collective key or a device grant if _token-secrets has no cached map.

A later start without a supplied secret still migrates, moving only the keys _token-secrets lacks. An oauth-resolved-admins cached by a supplied start stays in use, so a flat copy stays in the user vault. Serve does not delete a key it did not copy from a vault that may be shared, and design/primitives/serve.md says so.

No flag or stored key name changes, and no keys move for existing deployments that do not supply a client.

Upgrade note

A deployment that supplies a client, still has a flat oauth-resolved-admins, and has no cached map or collective k

𝗗 release v20261103.172234.0-sha.b94358f0𝗖𝗟 b94358f0𝗣 #3591𝗔 lab#4352

Athe HTTP read route hands each reply to the response writer in 64 KiB chunks, so a reply the client has not read keeps its slot (swamp-club#4349) (#3588)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-03T16:50:42Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): the HTTP read route hands each reply to the response writer in 64 KiB chunks, so a reply the client has not read keeps its slot (swamp-club#4349) (#3588)

Summary

POST /api/v1/request caps each principal at MAX_ACTIVE_REQUESTS (100) unread replies, but the cap did not hold. holdingSlotUntilRead freed a slot when its stream reported done. replyStream enqueues each NDJSON frame as one chunk, so Deno's response pump took a whole multi-MB reply in a single pull. The next pull saw done and released the slot before the client had read a byte. Any authenticated caller could therefore pile up unread replies, and serve's memory with them: 400 unread 12 MB replies pushed RSS to 7 GB in the issue's reproduction.

holdingSlotUntilRead now enqueues at most 64 KiB per pull, as subarrays of the frame's buffer with no copy. The pump stops pulling while its bounded buffers are full, so the stream end, and the release, comes only once the TCP peer has taken all but those buffers. The split is in the outermost stream, so plain and gzip replies are both covered. Memory for unread replies is again bounded by the cap times the reply size per principal. A single reply is still held whole, which is unchanged.

design/primitives/serve.md now says what the slot tracks: delivery to the TCP peer. A buffering reverse proxy counts as the peer.

Test Plan

  • Unit tests (src/serve/http_request_route_test.ts):
  • A 1 MiB reply, and a gzip reply o

Aa lock waiter no longer freezes swamp on Windows by spawning tasklist on every retry (swamp-club#4337) (#3586)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-03T16:19:22Zprocessfix

What's Changed

  • fix(process): a lock waiter no longer freezes swamp on Windows by spawning tasklist on every retry (swamp-club#4337) (#3586)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#4337. Parallel workflow steps on one behavior instance lost seconds per lock handoff on Windows, and the step holding the lock ran several times slower while others waited.

Every lock retry asks whether the holder is still alive (clearStaleHolder → isProcessGone). On Windows that check ran tasklist with outputSync(), which freezes the whole process. When the holder is the same process, which it is for parallel steps in one run, every waiter's retry also froze the holder's work.

  • Own pid: isProcessGone answers alive for this process's own pid without probing. It always answered alive; now it no longer pays for a tasklist spawn (or a libc dlopen on Linux) to learn it. This alone removes every check from the reported scenario.
  • Windows check: asks kernel32 in process (OpenProcess + GetExitCodeProcess), using the same cached-loader pattern as windows_job.ts. It falls back to tasklist only for a pid it cannot open, or when kernel32 cannot be loaded; the load failure is cached.

The Windows check goes beyond the reported scenario. Every Windows liveness check (run tracker, pending-pid rows, v3 presence, waits on another process's lock) stops spawning tasklist on each call. That's deliberate: serve freezes the same way when it waits on another process. Uncertain cases

  • Every frame is streamed back as one NDJSON line with no size cap, gzipped when the client accepts it.
  • Security:
  • The route is dispatched after validateNoAuthApiRequest, like every /api route, and a rule test pins that order.
  • It accepts bearer tokens only, through authenticateHttpCaller, which moved out of the datastore API so both now share it. No cookie-authenticated POST exists.

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-03T13:47:27Zworkerfix

What's Changed

  • fix(worker): a pending dispatch is built through the same schema its end transitions read with, so a __proto__ label key no longer makes the methods and the pure functions disagree (swamp-club#4332, swamp-club#4331) (#3583)

Summary

The property test pending dispatch transitions: the methods write exactly what the pure functions return failed whenever fc.dictionary generated a label key named __proto__ (seeds 598987396 and -1048400399).

startPendingDispatch copied the enqueue args into the stored record with a spread, which keeps an own __proto__ key. Each end transition read the record back through PendingDispatchSchema, and its Zod record parse drops that key. So the record enqueue wrote and the one an end transition wrote disagreed. startPendingDispatch now builds the waiting record through the same schema, so enqueue, every end transition, and serve's PendingDispatchLedger store the same shape.

Users can't reach this: workflow placement labels already go through PlacementFieldsSchema, which drops the key before anything is queued.

Changes

  • startPendingDispatch returns PendingDispatchSchema.parse({ ...args, state: "waiting" }).
  • The first-end-wins property compares against the schema-parsed record. The label arbitrary is unchanged.
  • New example test: an own __proto__ label plus a gpu label, through enqueue and then timeout. It fails on the old code.

Step starts

𝗗 release v20261102.235047.0-sha.5f4427f0𝗖𝗟 5f4427f0𝗣 #3580𝗔 lab#4296

Aread a behavior's declared additionalFiles at each remote dispatch, so a newly declared file ships without a serve restart (swamp-club#4282) (#3579)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-02T23:48:41Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): read a behavior's declared additionalFiles at each remote dispatch, so a newly declared file ships without a serve restart (swamp-club#4282) (#3579)

Summary

swamp serve reload never re-read extension manifests, so a file newly added to a manifest's additionalFiles didn't reach remote workers until serve restarted. Two gaps caused this:

  • resolveExtensionAssets cached each manifest's additionalFiles in a module-level extensionAssetsCache that nothing cleared. A re-registered type got the stale list back.
  • A manifest-only edit never re-registered the type at all. The local source refresher only re-registers when the source fingerprint changes, and that fingerprint covers TS modules, not manifest.yaml.

Clearing the cache on reload (the issue's suggestion) would fix only the first gap, and it would also miss an in-place upgrade of a pulled extension. Instead, the definition now carries a reader, and DispatchService.#ensureBundle calls it at each dispatch, next to the asset bytes it already reads there. The declared list, the fingerprint and the data plane's allowlist then all reflect the manifest as it is on disk when the step dispatches. No reload or restart is needed.

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-02T23:12:55Zpersistencefix

What's Changed

  • fix(persistence): append-only run index with bounded inputs and a cross-process lock (swamp-club#4235, swamp-club#4224) (#3575)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#4235 and swamp-club#4224.

Every workflow-run save read, parsed and rewrote the whole per-workflow .runs-index.json, and each entry kept the run's full inputs. With large inputs (a webhook body, a 155 KB intent) the index reached hundreds of MB. Saves blocked serve's event loop for seconds, serve ran out of heap, and past V8's ~512 MB string limit the index could not be written at all.

  • Append-only index (schema v5). The filename stays the same. The file is a header line {version, compactedBytes}, then one line per change ({id, entry} or {id, removed}); the last line for a run wins. A save appends its line without reading the index. Retention, GC and datastore pulls append removal lines. A save that leaves the file larger than max(256 KiB, 2 × compactedBytes) compacts it. Reads decode 1 MiB at a time in 4 ms slices of the event loop. A torn line marks the index stale, and the repair pass rewrites it. A legacy index is detected from its first 4 KiB and rebuilt without being decoded.
  • Bounded inputs. An index entry keeps input values up to 1 KiB each and 8 KiB per run, and lists the other keys in inputsOmitted. Run search and history search rows carry the bounded inputs and inputsOmitted. An --input filter on an omitted key, or a history --filter that reads inputs, reads th

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-02T22:41:35Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): every post-drain shutdown stage takes its bound from the shutdown deadline and is named when it stalls (swamp-club#4286) (#3572)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#4286. A serve with a large filesystem datastore finished its drain in 11 s, then logged nothing for six minutes until systemd killed it. Serve documents shutdown as bounded by the drain timeout plus about 25 s.

Cause. ShutdownDeadline bounded only three stages: the interrupt push, the stopped heartbeat and the v3 replication stop. Every other post-drain stage awaited its work in flight with no limit:

  • the GC services' dispose() spun on while (this.#running)
  • the pollers waited for a pull already running
  • the continuation sweep, revalidation services and telemetry flush awaited their pending promises
  • the run-index warm-up's workflow listing ignored its stop signal

None of these stages logged, so the journal could not name the one that held shutdown. The likely culprit is the worker GC sweep: it shares the retention pass's schedule, logs only when it deletes something, and runs uncancellable catalog queries.

1. ShutdownStages (src/serve/shutdown_stages.ts). Runs each post-drain stage in turn and never rejects. Each stage resolves to done, failed or timed_out, and has one of three bounds:

  • left (default): races the stage against what the deadline has left; once the budget is spent it gets nothing.
  • floor: always gets at least 0.5 s.
  • **`awaited

Aworker reads that can outgrow a control frame move to the data plane, and every control frame is capped below Deno's 64 MiB receive limit (swamp-club#4234) (#3571)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-02T22:21:45Zremotefix

What's Changed

  • fix(remote): worker reads that can outgrow a control frame move to the data plane, and every control frame is capped below Deno's 64 MiB receive limit (swamp-club#4234) (#3571)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#4234. An enrolled worker kept disconnecting with control socket closed: Frame too large, failing every in-flight dispatch on it (write-then-drop for steps that had written data).

Cause. Deno's WebSocket receiver rejects any frame of 67,108,864 bytes (64 MiB) or more with Frame too large and closes the socket. Nothing can raise the limit. Serve sent capability replies with no size check, and two of them have no upper bound:

  • queryData, used by readModelData (with attributes, across every version) and dataQueryService.query
  • the readOutput list reads (findAll, findByDefinition)

A small dispatch could get a reply over the limit, and that one reply took down the shared control socket. Reproduced on Deno 2.9.8: a 67,108,863-byte frame is delivered, while a 67,108,864-byte frame closes the socket with exactly the reported error.

1. Large reads move to the HTTP data plane (protocol version 7).

  • New POST /data/query and POST /data/outputs routes call the same CapabilityService.queryData and readOutput as the control verbs. Visibility and refusal rules are therefore shared, and errors go through the same sanitizeRpcError.
  • Each route takes the dispatch from the credential (never from the body) and caps the request body a

Changes

Cancel (workflow_cancel.ts, model_cancel.ts):

  • On a direct v3 binding, cancel first finds its runs through a read-only open.
  • It stops the owners the run tracker vouches for (stopOwnersBeforeWriter, stopLocalRunOwners, stopMethodRunOwners).
  • Only then does it take the writer and settle the records. The stopped pids are passed on, so no owner is stopped twice.
  • Behavior/model cancel takes the writer only when an owner died without saving its method run.
  • A cancel never answers writer_busy. If another process holds the writer

2026-11-02T20:47:02Zworkflowsfix

What's Changed

  • fix(workflows): a run another job suspended leaves a level's unstarted steps pending once the abort fired, so a lone step is no longer started under it (swamp-club#4179) (#3566)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#4179.

#4149 made a step level reached after the abort start no work, but it turned that rule off when another job has suspended the run, so the run keeps its pending steps for the resume. merge() passes a single stream through whatever the signal. So in a suspended run with an aborted signal, a level of two or more steps stayed pending, but a lone step started under the cancelled signal. A real method then fails as stopped-by-abort, which reopenAbortedWork does not reopen after approval. This breaks the "No step is invoked with the cancelled signal" invariant.

  • When it happens: the issue traced a *later* level of a job. That level can't be reached, because runJobInSpan stops walking a job once the run is suspended, with no await before the next level. The level that is exposed is a job's first step level. A job that starts beside a sibling that suspended reaches it after the abort when its forEach expansion reads a vault, since that is real I/O before the level starts.
  • Fix (runJobInSpan): when the run is suspended and the step's signal has already aborted, a step yet to start gets no stream and stays pending, whatever the level's size. A forEach iteration with no record gets a pending one, so the job stays resumable. Finished

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): a pending-dispatch transition writes the queue entry's record directly, so a queued step no longer leaves output records behind (swamp-club#4292) (#3564)

Summary

When a remote step waits for a worker slot, serve records the queue entry under swamp/pending-dispatch: enqueue when the step queues, then mark_dispatched, timeout or cancel when it leaves the queue. Each transition ran the method, and every run saved an output record and a run log. A saturated pool therefore wrote about two output records per queued step, and by default nothing pruned them.

Serve now writes the queue entry's record directly, the same way StepLeaseLedger handles leases (swamp-club#4038) and WorkerStatusLedger handles worker status (swamp-club#4285):

  • src/serve/pending_dispatch_ledger.ts (new). PendingDispatchLedger reads the record, applies the pure transition that the methods also use, and writes the next version with the same resource reader and writer a method run uses. The write happens inside runInRootUnitOfWork(ctx, { flush: undefined }). Until the pending definition exists, a transition falls back to the method run. Transitions that arrive while that fallback is creating the definition wait for it and then write directly. If the fallback fails, the next waiter takes over.
  • pending_dispatch_model.ts. The method bodies become pure functions that both the methods and the ledger call: startPendingDispatch, `endedPendingDis

What's Changed

  • fix(test): the extractContentMetadata property test no longer generates a block comment that closes early (swamp-club#4183) (#3562)

Cause. The block-comment branch of literalStatementArb deleted each */ from its noise in a single pass. The extractor is unchanged. Affected commands: none.
