Summary

Every workflow run that saved a new auto-definition moved its type directory's stat. Serve's DefinitionIdIndex then re-listed the whole directory, stat'ed every file in it, and unindexed and re-indexed every entry. Inside the 2 s racy window, each further check did all of that again. Serve CPU per run therefore grew with the number of auto-definitions of that type, and so with every run ever made (swamp-club#4307, a follow-up to #4280).

What changed (src/infrastructure/persistence/definition_id_index.ts)

  • Kept files. A relist keeps a regular <name>.yaml without a stat or a read when the index already held it declaring that name with a settled stat. That is how a save names a definition whose name is filename-safe, and it is the trust AutoDefinitionNameIndex already places in such files. These are still stat'ed and read as before:
  • new names
  • symlinks
  • broken or unparsed files
  • legacy <id>.yaml files
  • files whose stat was too recent to trust
  • Commits touch only what changed. #commit unindexes only entries that changed between the two listings. Indexing every entry is a no-op for one still indexed.
  • Verifying kept files. One shared lstat pass checks the kept files, in two cases:
  • On a miss. A lookup by id that finds nothing runs the

Athe read model's catalog catch-up and compacted-base staging prepare each SQLite statement once per connection, not once per row (swamp-club#4304) (#3559)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-02T18:50:45Zdatastorefix

What's Changed

  • fix(datastore): the read model's catalog catch-up and compacted-base staging prepare each SQLite statement once per connection, not once per row (swamp-club#4304) (#3559)

Summary

Catching up a partition of 7,000 data versions prepared about 45,000 SQLite statements inside one synchronous transaction, because CatalogStore's batched writer called db.prepare for every statement of every catalog action. Preparing a statement in Deno's node:sqlite contends on a lock shared by every isolate in the process. Under deno test --parallel the commit therefore stalled for over a minute, blocked rather than computing.

  • CatalogStore caches the statements its batched writer (applyInTransaction) reaches, per connection. vacuum() empties the cache when it closes the old connection, and so does close(). SQL and parameters are unchanged.
  • ReadModelState.stageBase prepares its six compacted-base staging INSERTs once at construction, like the class's other statements.

Measurements

The real CatalogStore applying 7,000 upserts in one transaction:

| | alone | 32 in-process workers |

|---|---|---|

| before | 454ms | 80.7s |

| after | 90ms | 4.0s |

The "before" numbers match the issue's 467ms / 85.2s. Production serve runs SQLite on one isolate (src/ creates no Web Workers), so it gains the single-thread speedup and never hit the in-process cliff.

A 45x slowdown remains under 32 in-process workers. It comes from other per-call work inside SQLite

2026-11-02T15:55:54Zservefix

What's Changed

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#4287. Serve's worker GC waited a full interval (1 hour) before its first sweep, and that sweep is the only thing that reaps ended step leases, pending dispatches and spent enrollment tokens (swamp run gc and swamp data gc leave serve bookkeeping alone by design). A serve restarted more often than hourly never swept, so step-lease records grew without bound: the reporter saw data/swamp/step-lease go from about 7,000 to over 70,000 files in about three hours.

  • WorkerGcService takes an optional initialDelayMs, the same shape as RetentionGcService, and serve sets it to 5 minutes (DEFAULT_WORKER_GC_INITIAL_DELAY_MS). Later sweeps stay hourly; without the option the first sweep still waits one interval.
  • Serve's shutdown waits for a running sweep, and the bookkeeping reap read every lease, dispatch and token body before deleting anything, with no stop check. Now that a sweep often runs while serve restarts, listCandidates checks isStopping before the enrollment-token lookup, each kind's query and each body read, and once it is true it returns no candidates. A large backlog no longer holds up serve's exit; the next sweep lists again.
  • design/enablers/remote-execution.md describes the new timing and what a shutdown stops.

Safety checks done while planning:

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-02T15:44:21Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): a save or checkpoint that outlasts the shutdown write fence wait no longer lands over the interrupted record (swamp-club#4171) (#3547)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#4171, a follow-up to #4101's run write fence. A late write by the executor could still overwrite the interrupted record that serve's shutdown saved and pushed, in three ways:

1. A save that stalled before writing. saveRun passed the fence check, then waited in beforeSave (closing signal waits on a slow store) for longer than the fence's 2 s wait, then wrote cancelled over interrupted. This is most likely during a shutdown with a degraded datastore, which is the case the fence was built for.

2. A v3 checkpoint that read the record before shutdown wrote it. The read happens outside the partition lock and a run put carries no expectation, so it committed running at the next seq.

3. A v3 checkpoint that failed before shutdown. It kept its stored op, and the executor's end commit replayed it.

The fence is now checked at the last point each write can still be stopped:

  • YamlWorkflowRunRepository.save takes RunSaveOptions { fence, onRefused } and checks isClosed right before atomicWriteTextFile, after beforeSave. A refused save writes, stages, indexes and publishes nothing.
  • A run put carries a withdrawn check. appendGroup asks it under the partition lock on every attempt, drops withdrawn ops, and discards an outbox entry an earlier attempt prepar

Aa v3 reader never replays over a live writer that left the read model incomplete, and an unlistable directory or a case-sensitive root no longer wedges or misreads catch-up (swamp-club#4277) (#3542)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-02T06:12:12Zdatastorefix

What's Changed

  • fix(datastore): a v3 reader never replays over a live writer that left the read model incomplete, and an unlistable directory or a case-sensitive root no longer wedges or misreads catch-up (swamp-club#4277) (#3542)

Summary

Fixes the three solo v3 review findings in swamp-club#4277.

  • A reader replays over a live writer. A writer's reset, or its compacted-base load ahead of a partition catch-up, clears the read model's complete mark mid-command. A reader then replayed older commits over the writer's newer files. The read model now records the pid of the process that cleared the mark, as incomplete_pid: an integer in the existing meta table, so no schema change. A reader whose live writer left it incomplete reads the files as they are.
  • An unlistable directory wedges catch-up. In list mode, PermissionDenied and NotFound from a directory listing are treated as unchecked, with one warning per directory, instead of throwing out of every catch-up. An alias is reported only when another key's path reaches the same file (same device and inode), so a case-sensitive root keeps vpc and VPC apart. An undecidable stat counts as an alias, so the write is skipped and the catch-up never stops.
  • A failed closing sync hides owed commits. This can't happen: only serve's upkeep fills the owed set, and any commit serve makes moves its commit mark. Replication tests with a real sidecar loss pin both cases, and design/enablers/datastores.md rec

  • A row serve owns refuses with the --server hint, whatever the record says. A forged record that leaves out instanceId can no longer reach a local serve's pid.
  • A local row under another pid is either a local resume about to hand its row over (it does so right after releasing the run's claim), or a run anot

1. More than 64 referencing partitions. CommitStore.partitionsReferencing gains an after cursor, and serve pages through every referencing partition.

2. A superseded definition. A definition's config partition vouches in three steps:

  • a live record, as on main;
  • then its stored commits, newest first, decided as GET commits and the changes feed decide them;
  • then its compacted base records, decided as GET base decides them (new CommitStore.baseRecordKeysNaming).

3. An incomplete read model. Live records are skipped while the read model catches up, never waited on, and history decides alone.

  • Partition budget runs out: GET chunks answers 503 chunk_access_undecided, naming no partition. has leaves the hash out, and only writers ask it,

What's Changed

  • fix(datastore): a forwarded run record applies decisions serve holds and reaches nothing outside its own run (swamp-club#4245) (#3536)

Summary

Forwarded run records now apply decisions serve holds, and can never make one. Serve does not try to stop a member forging the record of a run they drive; it stops that forgery reaching anything outside their own run. The approved threat model is in design/enablers/access-control.md, "Run records: the threat model".

Fixes swamp-club#4245, swamp-club#4044, swamp-club#4045 and swamp-club#4046.

The four guarantees:

1. No forged decisions.

2. Runs serve drives take no forwarded record at all.

3. Serve never acts on a member-driven run's state with anyone else's authority.

4. Serve records who wrote each record.

Out of scope: the statuses, outputs and timings a member reports for a run they drive, splitting a report across commits, and initiatedBy / triggerSource. For a gate that must bind its runner, run the workflow on serve (--server, a trigger or a schedule).

The status, allowance and skip rules are gone.

𝗗 release v20261102.031954.0-sha.482baaa4𝗖𝗟 482baaa4𝗣 #3536

Athe writer's replica check runs on a new lineage, a new snapshot or a 4-hour backstop, not every 10 minutes, and restores beside commits.db (swamp-club#4261) (#3534)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-02T02:23:19Zdatastore-v3fix

What's Changed

  • fix(datastore-v3): the writer's replica check runs on a new lineage, a new snapshot or a 4-hour backstop, not every 10 minutes, and restores beside commits.db (swamp-club#4261) (#3534)

Summary

The v3 writer's sidecar checked its replica by doing a full restore of commits.db up to every 10 minutes while its writer kept acknowledging, into /tmp/swamp-<uid>. For a serve that writes all day that is about 144 full downloads a day (about 14 GB on a 100 MB commits.db), and a whole extra copy in memory on a tmpfs /tmp. A developer's run of CLI sidecars paid one restore per idle exit.

Fixes swamp-club#4261.

When a check runs now. Only when the acknowledged TXID is not covered by the last verdict, and only on one of these triggers:

  • a new lineage, which has no verdict yet;
  • a new level-9 snapshot the last verdict did not read through. The sidecar learns this from litestream ltx -level 9 -json, one LIST of about 1 KB, at most every 15 minutes and only while something is uncovered;
  • the 4-hour backstop, counted from the recorded verdict's checkedAt, so a fresh sidecar doesn't check just because it started;
  • idle exit, only when one of the triggers above holds.

Retries and idle exit. A check that recorded no verdict (bucket unreachable, deadline hit) retries 10 minutes after it ended. An idle sidecar never re-checks a TXID it already tried, so a slow or broken bucket can't keep a CLI sidecar alive.

𝗗 release v20261102.021849.0-sha.0f595b35𝗖𝗟 0f595b35𝗣 #3534𝗣 #3935𝗔 lab#4261

Aa serve-mode checkout never keeps a dropped unit's save over a commit it sent, in a catch-up or a recovery a root waits on (swamp-club#4257) (#3533)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-02T00:01:30Zdatastorefix

What's Changed

  • fix(datastore): a serve-mode checkout never keeps a dropped unit's save over a commit it sent, in a catch-up or a recovery a root waits on (swamp-club#4257) (#3533)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#4257, a follow-up to swamp-club#4249 (#3530) raised by that PR's adversarial review. In a serve-mode checkout (format 3, serverAddress set), #3530's own-work skip (holdsOwnWork) applied whenever any unit in the process was open, and in every replay. Two edges let it keep content that never committed.

Edge 1: a root waiting on recovery.

  • runRoot counts itself as open before it waits on units.recovering.
  • A root that arrived while recoverDroppedWork was replaying therefore made the replay skip the dropped unit's checkpoint.
  • The dropped save stayed in the file, and the sweep of unowned files left it there.

Edge 2: another root open.

  • Unit A sent checkpoint X, then dropped its later save while root B was open.
  • A catch-up during B applied X to state and catalog but left A's dropped save in the file.
  • Until teardown, B read content that never committed.

The fix.

  • A dropped unit forgets the commits it sent.
  • Each unit notes the txnId and partition of every commit before handing it to the sink, so an append that fails but lands anyway is still counted.
  • When the unit drops, UnitTracker.dropped reports those txnIds and the v3 handle removes them from units.sent. A catch-up then puts the checkpoint back over the dropped save.
𝗗 release v20261101.235533.0-sha.d748e1dc𝗖𝗟 d748e1dc𝗣 #3533𝗣 #3530𝗔 lab#4257

Aa takeover holds back only writes to a key whose kept lease is not adopted yet, an accept keeps the content until its change replicates, and deposits are bounded before they are parsed (swamp-club#4258) (#3532)

`fix` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-01T23:02:44Zservefix

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): a takeover holds back only writes to a key whose kept lease is not adopted yet, an accept keeps the content until its change replicates, and deposits are bounded before they are parsed (swamp-club#4258) (#3532)

Summary

Follow-ups to swamp-club#4195 in serve's datastore API (swamp-club#4258).

1. A takeover no longer stalls every write. While a new leader adopts kept leases, a commit, lease request, forced release, renewal, release or quarantine accept waits (503 serve_not_leader) only when its own key's kept lease is not adopted yet. While the kept leases cannot be listed, every write still waits. The lease listing waits for the whole adoption, since until then it would leave out the leases not adopted yet. A commit and an accept are checked again under the forwarded-commit lock. An adoption retry never holds back a lease granted while adopting.

2. An accept confirms replication before it marks the change accepted and drops the held content. Otherwise it answers a retryable 503 and the change stays held. An accept of a change that already landed (unconfirmed replication, or a stop before marking) is confirmed and marked without landing it again. That only happens when the landed commit is the author's; otherwise it is refused and stays held.

3. Deposits are bounded before parsing. A deposit takes one of its caller's upload slots (MAX_CONCURRENT_CHUNK_REQUESTS, shared with commits and chunk uploads) before its body is read.

What's Changed

  • fix(datastore): a serve-mode checkout's catch-up never puts a run's own checkpoint back over its later save, so a run that reports suspended commits suspended (swamp-club#4249) (#3530)

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#4249. A regression from swamp-club#4120 (PR #3483), which added serve's feed watch: first shipped in v20261029.223622.0-sha.c5b6107b.

The bug. In a serve-mode checkout (format 3, serverAddress set), workflow run --json on a workflow with a manual_approval step could print suspended while the run's record, in the checkout's copy and at serve, stayed running. Serve then refused to approve the run.

The cause.

  • A workflow run checkpoints its new record to serve mid-run, saves it again as suspended, and commits that last save when its root ends, reading the record's file.
  • workflow run watches serve's feed meanwhile. A frame bringing the run's own checkpoint ran the checkout's catch-up between the suspended save and the root's commit.
  • The read model wrote the checkpoint (running) over the file, and the root committed running. A trace of a source build caught this order; serve's commit log held the root's commit with the checkpoint's content.
  • Both copies hold running: the wrong record is what serve committed, not a stale local copy.

The fix.

  • Noting own commits. A serve-mode checkout's v3 handle notes the txnId of each commit it sends to serve, before sending it, so a feed frame that beats serve's rep

`perf` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-05T02:08:24Zserveperf

What's Changed

  • perf(serve): a team member's write no longer pays a bucket read of its token secret per request, repeat catch-ups or one-at-a-time chunk fetches, so its commit bound drops from about 4 s to under 1 s at 100 ms per storage call (swamp-club#4401) (#3624)

Summary

After swamp-club#4387 a team member's write cost a flat ~4 s at 100 ms per storage call, against K2a's 500 ms bar. A latency lane with spans on both sides showed where it went. Every request a member sends waits for the one before it, and for each one serve read the member's token secret from the bucket. That was two thirds of a write's time. The rest was repeated catch-ups, chunks fetched one at a time, and a confirmation marker rewritten on every sync.

  • Token check. Serve remembers for a minute that a token's vault secret matched the record's fingerprint, then checks the presented secret against that fingerprint. It keeps no secret.
  • The record is still read on every request, so revocation, expiry and a re-mint act on the next one.
  • A record without a fingerprint always reads the vault.
  • A hit refuses a wrong secret as secret-mismatch, as before.
  • Confirmation. A lineage's confirmed.json is written once per process, keyed by datastore, replica root and fence.
  • Catch-ups. A member catches up from serve once at the writer hold and once under the lock. The repository open and the lock's second pass bring only the read model up.
  • Chunk prefetch. A read-model page

`perf` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-03T17:12:42Zserveperf

What's Changed

  • perf(serve): an extension workflow lookup parses only the matched file, so authorizing a run no longer parses every extension workflow per request (swamp-club#4341) (#3590)

Summary

Authorizing a run of an extension workflow (history.get / history.logs) parsed every extension workflow file on every request, about 760ms p50 on ops. run.search repeated the same walk. swamp-club#4341.

ExtensionWorkflowRepository now keeps the same kind of stat-validated listing cache that YamlWorkflowRepository already has:

  • Lookups. findByName and findById stat every file. They re-read only the matched file and accept it only if it still has the requested name or id *and* the name the scan used to decide shadowing. Anything else falls back to findAll. A parsed Workflow is never cached.
  • Listings. The new findAllListings parses nothing that hasn't changed, so CompositeWorkflowRepository.findAllListings and run.search stop parsing every file.
  • Broken files. Broken and non-workflow YAML is cached as skipped and never claims a name, matching findAll's dedupe. A broken file with an unchanged stat is not parsed again.
  • What gets cached. A listing is kept only for files older than a 2s racy window, and paths a scan no longer sees are pruned.
  • Symlinks. A symlinked workflow file is followed on the re-read, as findAll follows it.
  • Per-request view. memoizedView shares one cheap scan per request instead of one full par

2026-11-02T15:26:20Zserveperf

What's Changed

  • perf(serve): a definition's owner lookup answers from the index while the file's stat has not moved, so a repeat request reads no definition file (swamp-club#4231) (#3546)

Summary

Every request that names its outputs used to re-read each owning definition file. On ops that's 10,394 files, about 2.7s and ~30% of model.usage.summary. Now DefinitionIdIndex keeps each file's owner fields (id, name, tags, type) beside its location, filled wherever it reads the file. A lookup by id takes one lstat per owning file. It answers from the index only when that stat:

  • matches the one it kept,
  • is settled outside the 2s racy window, and
  • reports a ctime or an inode.

Otherwise it reads the file, as before. A symlink is always read, so its target is still checked to be inside the repo.

  • Narrower port. DefinitionOwnerView.findAllById now returns a DefinitionOwner holding only the fields naming and authorization read. No executable definition is kept between requests. findByName still reads every time, since its caller runs the definition.
  • Changes are seen on the next request. That covers in-place edits, rename-over, deletion, and a deny-by-tag grant. A read that still declares the id also re-indexes its name.
  • No state leaks between requests. Returned tags are copies. No owner is kept for a path that is a symlink now.
  • Read counts change in some tests. The swamp-club#4141 and #4150 handler tests now move each owner's stat, so th

Change (untyped) · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-11T01:30:24Z

What's Changed

  • fix(serve): webhook intake and run admission refuse in the same turn /ready reports shutting_down, so no delivery is acknowledged and then dropped during shutdown (swamp-club#4629) (#3738)

Summary

During swamp serve shutdown, /ready could answer 503 shutting_down while the webhook route still answered 200 {"status":"queued"}. The shutdown handler set shuttingDown and then awaited the pid-file removal and the pending replay sweep stop before WebhookService.drain() set its own draining flag. A delivery in that window was acknowledged, then dropped from the queue and left pending until the next boot, and the sender had no reason to retry. Seen in swamp-uat (tests/cli/serve/none/lifecycle_test.ts).

Regression from #3706 (sweep-stop await before the drain), widened by #3716 (pid-file await before beginDraining).

  • WebhookService gains an optional shuttingDown predicate; both intake checks (entry, and the re-check after verification and hooks) refuse when draining or the predicate is true.
  • serve wires it as () => admission.phase === "draining" and calls admission.beginDraining() in the same turn as shuttingDown = true, before any await, with an invariant comment at the line.
  • /ready, WebSocket and action-route admission, and webhook intake now switch together. drain(), the queue, replay and the shutdown stage order are unchanged.
  • design/primitives/serve.md states the guarantee.

Test Plan

  • New unit tests in

```

TypeError: the source path is neither a regular file nor a symlink to a regular file: copy '<tmp>/repo/moved/data' -> '<tmp>/repo/elsewhere/data'

𝗗 release v20261104.163022.0-sha.ed274a65𝗖𝗟 ed274a65𝗣 #3609𝗔 lab#4389

Cpin that a cached extension archive is authorized by the registry checksum request (swamp-club#4350) (#3589)

`test` change · swamp-club/swamp

2026-11-03T16:58:30Zextensionstest

What's Changed

  • test(extensions): pin that a cached extension archive is authorized by the registry checksum request (swamp-club#4350) (#3589)

Summary

Resolves swamp-club#4350. The host-wide extension archive cache from swamp-club#4339 skips /download on a hit and keeps only the authenticated GET /api/v1/extensions/<name>@<version>/checksum. The issue asked whether a cache shared by several identities (CI runners, swamp serve, a user who switches accounts) could hand out an archive the caller is no longer entitled to, or a version that was yanked after it was cached.

It cannot. swamp-club's checksum handler applies the same gates as download: canViewPrivateExtension for private and collective extensions (404 when not entitled), and 410 for a yanked extension or a yanked version. No middleware or billing gate applies to /download alone. On the client, a refused checksum (401 or 410) throws and fails the install, and a missing one (404) bypasses the cache, so the download, which is refused too, fails the install.

This PR records that dependency and pins it:

  • Tests in src/libswamp/extensions/pull_archive_cache_test.ts, each with the correct archive already cached:
  • a refused checksum fails the install before the cache is read;
  • a hidden (null) checksum never reads the cache, and the refused download fails the install;
  • every cache hit makes its own checksum request, so it is never memoized.
  • Comment on fetchArchive: the checksum

Summary

Fixes swamp-club#4278. Nothing tested what happens when a member's lease and commits go through different serve nodes in team mode. This adds that coverage. Only test code changes.

  • integration/serve_v3_team_lease_across_nodes_test.ts (new): two serve nodes, each run as a Worker, on one v3 datastore. It runs on filesystem v3 and on the bucket test double. Members take leases with the CLI's own explicit-lease client and send framed commits:

1. alice takes the lease through the leader and commits through the follower. The commit lands under her lease and fence.

2. The reverse: lease through the follower (forwarded), commit through the leader. The commit lands.

3. bob commits through the follower, with no lease and naming alice's lease id. Both are refused with commit_lease-lost, as through the leader, and the refusal never names alice. bob's renew and release of her lease are refused too.

4. alice renews and releases through the node she didn't lease through, each way round. After the release, bob's commit lands.

  • integration/serve_v3_team_bucket_node_worker.ts: the replica setting is now optional. Without one, the nodes share one commits.db, and a replica variable set in the shell is ignored. A new opt-in `leaderO
